```text
import { dbClient } from "@/server/db";
import { emitNamedEvent } from "@/server/events/named-events";
import { goalAcpDispatcher, type GoalAcpDispatchResult } from "@/server/runs/goal-acp";
import { goalControl, type GoalControlMethod } from "@/server/runs/goal-control";
import { goalOutboxDispatcher } from "@/server/runs/goal-outbox";
import { redactGoalErrorMessage } from "@/server/runs/goal-errors";
import type { GoalMutationAction, GoalSnapshot } from "@/shared/goal-plan";

type DispatchDependencies = {
  getGoal(runId: string): Promise<GoalSnapshot | null>;
  isControlSettled(snapshot: GoalSnapshot): Promise<boolean>;
  dispatch(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, action: GoalMutationAction): Promise<GoalAcpDispatchResult>;
  markControlApplied(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, method: GoalControlMethod, action: GoalMutationAction): Promise<boolean>;
};

export type GoalControlDispatchOutcome =
  | (GoalAcpDispatchResult & { snapshot: GoalSnapshot; action: GoalMutationAction })
  | { kind: "already_applied"; snapshot: GoalSnapshot; action: GoalMutationAction }
  | { kind: "superseded"; snapshot: GoalSnapshot | null; action: GoalMutationAction; reason: "goal_absent" | "state_churn" };

function sameFence(left: GoalSnapshot, right: GoalSnapshot) {
  return left.goalId === right.goalId
    && left.revision === right.revision
    && left.leaseGeneration === right.leaseGeneration
    && left.workerId === right.workerId
    && left.acpSessionId === right.acpSessionId;
}

export function recoveryAction(snapshot: GoalSnapshot): GoalMutationAction {
  if (snapshot.status === "cleared") return "clear";
  if (snapshot.status === "paused") return "pause";
  return "set";
}

export class GoalControlDispatchCoordinator {
  private readonly tails = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();
  /**
   * Controls the agent refused because it was mid-turn, keyed by run.
   *
   * A busy agent is the one dispatch failure that resolves on its own, so the
   * request is parked here instead of being recorded as a failure, and replayed
   * once the turn settles. Losing the map on restart is safe: a deferred
   * control leaves `control_method` NULL, which is exactly what
   * `recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup` re-dispatches.
   */
  private readonly deferredByRun = new Map<string, { snapshot: GoalSnapshot; action: GoalMutationAction }>();

  constructor(private readonly dependencies: DispatchDependencies) {}

  dispatch(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, action: GoalMutationAction): Promise<GoalControlDispatchOutcome> {
    return this.enqueue(snapshot.runId, () => this.converge(snapshot, action));
  }

  hasDeferredControl(runId: string) {
    return this.deferredByRun.has(runId);
  }

  retryDeferredControl(runId: string): Promise<GoalControlDispatchOutcome> | null {
    const deferred = this.deferredByRun.get(runId);
    if (!deferred) return null;
    return this.dispatch(deferred.snapshot, deferred.action);
  }

  private async enqueue<T>(runId: string, operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
    const previous = this.tails.get(runId) ?? Promise.resolve();
    const result = previous.catch(() => undefined).then(operation);
    const tail = result.then(() => undefined, () => undefined);
    this.tails.set(runId, tail);
    try {
      return await result;
    } finally {
      if (this.tails.get(runId) === tail) this.tails.delete(runId);
    }
  }

  private async converge(
    requestedSnapshot: GoalSnapshot,
    requestedAction: GoalMutationAction,
  ): Promise<GoalControlDispatchOutcome> {
    let latest: GoalSnapshot | null = null;
    // This pass supersedes whatever was parked for the run; it re-parks below
    // only if the agent is still mid-turn.
    this.deferredByRun.delete(requestedSnapshot.runId);
    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 8; attempt += 1) {
      latest = await this.dependencies.getGoal(requestedSnapshot.runId);
      if (!latest) {
        return { kind: "superseded", snapshot: null, action: requestedAction, reason: "goal_absent" };
      }
      const action = sameFence(latest, requestedSnapshot) ? requestedAction : recoveryAction(latest);
      if (await this.dependencies.isControlSettled(latest)) {
        return { kind: "already_applied", snapshot: latest, action };
      }
      const dispatched = await this.dependencies.dispatch(latest, action);
      const outcome = { ...dispatched, snapshot: latest, action } as GoalControlDispatchOutcome;
      if (dispatched.kind === "deferred" && dispatched.reason === "worker_busy") {
        this.deferredByRun.set(latest.runId, { snapshot: latest, action });
      }
      if (dispatched.kind !== "dispatched") return outcome;
      if (await this.dependencies.markControlApplied(latest, dispatched.method, action)) return outcome;
      // The canonical row changed while the provider call was in flight. Loop
      // under the same per-run queue and converge the provider to the newest
      // durable revision before a later request may dispatch.
    }
    return { kind: "superseded", snapshot: latest, action: recoveryAction(latest!), reason: "state_churn" };
  }
}

export function createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator(
  dependencies: DispatchDependencies = {
    getGoal: (runId) => goalControl.getGoal(runId),
    isControlSettled: (snapshot) => goalControl.isControlSettled(snapshot),
    dispatch: (snapshot, action) => goalAcpDispatcher.dispatch(snapshot, action),
    markControlApplied: (snapshot, method, action) => goalControl.markControlApplied(snapshot, method, action),
  },
) {
  return new GoalControlDispatchCoordinator(dependencies);
}

export const goalControlDispatchCoordinator = createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator();

/**
 * Replay a goal control the agent refused because it was mid-turn.
 *
 * Called when a turn settles, which is the moment the refusal stops being true.
 * A no-op for every run that has nothing parked, so it is safe on the live-sync
 * path that walks the whole catalog.
 */
export async function retryDeferredGoalControl(
  runId: string,
  coordinator: GoalControlDispatchCoordinator = goalControlDispatchCoordinator,
) {
  const pending = coordinator.retryDeferredControl(runId);
  if (!pending) return null;
  let outcome: GoalControlDispatchOutcome;
  try {
    outcome = await pending;
  } catch (error) {
    const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
    const snapshot = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);
    if (snapshot) await goalControl.recordControlFailure(snapshot, "transport", reason);
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
      runId,
      goalId: snapshot?.goalId ?? runId,
      workerId: snapshot?.workerId ?? null,
      reason,
    });
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "error.surfaced",
      code: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
      message: reason,
      surface: "banner",
      runId,
      ...(snapshot?.workerId ? { workerId: snapshot.workerId } : {}),
    });
    await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
    return null;
  }

  if (outcome.kind === "dispatched") {
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "goal.reconciliation.completed",
      runId,
      goalId: outcome.snapshot.goalId,
      // A dispatched control always holds a lease; `goal-acp` defers otherwise.
      workerId: outcome.snapshot.workerId ?? "",
      revision: outcome.snapshot.revision,
      leaseGeneration: outcome.snapshot.leaseGeneration,
    });
  } else if (outcome.kind !== "already_applied") {
    if (outcome.kind === "unsupported" && outcome.snapshot.status !== "cleared") {
      await goalControl.recordControlFailure(outcome.snapshot, "unsupported", outcome.reason);
    }
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "goal.reconciliation.refused",
      runId,
      goalId: outcome.snapshot?.goalId ?? runId,
      workerId: outcome.snapshot?.workerId ?? null,
      reason: outcome.reason,
    });
  }
  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
  return outcome;
}

export async function recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup() {
  const pending = await dbClient.execute(
    `SELECT run_id FROM run_goals
     WHERE control_method IS NULL AND worker_id IS NOT NULL AND acp_session_id IS NOT NULL
     ORDER BY updated_at ASC, run_id ASC`,
  );
  for (const row of pending.rows) {
    const runId = String(row.run_id);
    const snapshot = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);
    if (!snapshot) continue;
    try {
      const result = await goalControlDispatchCoordinator.dispatch(snapshot, recoveryAction(snapshot));
      if (result.kind === "unsupported" && result.snapshot.status !== "cleared") {
        await goalControl.recordControlFailure(result.snapshot, "unsupported", result.reason);
      }
    } catch (error) {
      const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
        runId,
        goalId: snapshot.goalId,
        workerId: snapshot.workerId,
        reason,
      });
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "error.surfaced",
        code: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
        message: reason,
        surface: "log",
        runId,
        workerId: snapshot.workerId ?? undefined,
      });
    }
  }
  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:46:29.182Z

Since the snapshot at rev 2 has both workerId and acpSessionId, the no_active_lease must stem from getAgent throwing not_found — meaning the bridge no longer hosts that worker, likely because the idle session got evicted after a long idle period or the runner restarted. So the user's prompt path probably needs to lazily resume or reattach the worker rather than assume it's still live.

I'm now wondering whether the goal-dispatch path treats a missing agent as "defer until next attach," and whether the goal actually got re-dispatched when the worker reattached at 20:43:53. The "Goal set: fix all of the bugs" message right after suggests either a /goal command fired on reattach, or the agent itself emitted that state — but there's no user_input event for a /goal call, so I need to check whether goal-state.ts re-dispatches the goal automatically on reattach, or whether Claude's ACP adapter generates that message from a session extension. I should look at goal-state.ts to see how it handles the reattach case and whether the plain "Fix all of the bugs" input got transformed into a goal command somewhere in the harness.

```text
  }
  // Derive before the lease is attached so the attach and the dispatch below

    const coordinator = options.dispatch

          isControlSettled: (goalSnapshot) => goalControl.isControlSettled(goalSnapshot),
          dispatch: (goalSnapshot) => options.dispatch!(goalSnapshot),
          markControlApplied: (goalSnapshot, method, action) => goalControl.markControlApplied(goalSnapshot, method, action),
        })
      : goalControlDispatchCoordinator;
    const dispatched = await coordinator.dispatch(snapshot, "set");
    if (dispatched.snapshot) settledSnapshot = dispatched.snapshot;
    if (dispatched.kind === "unsupported") {
        await goalControl.recordControlFailure(dispatched.snapshot, "unsupported", dispatched.reason);
        await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
        emitNamedEvent({

      });
      return { kind: "rejected" as const, reason: dispatched.reason };

  } catch (error) {

      await goalControl.recordControlFailure(snapshot, "transport", reason);
      await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
      emitNamedEvent({ kind: "goal.reconciliation.failed", runId, goalId: snapshot.goalId, workerId, reason });
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "error.surfaced",
        code: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
        message: reason,
        surface: "banner",
        runId,
        workerId,
      });
      return { kind: "rejected" as const, reason };
  }
  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();
  emitNamedEvent({

    goalId: settledSnapshot.goalId,
    workerId,
    revision: settledSnapshot.revision,
    leaseGeneration: settledSnapshot.leaseGeneration,
  });
  return { kind: "accepted" as const, snapshot: settledSnapshot };
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:47:13.214Z

I'm wondering if manager.ts actually runs in the separate bridge process, which could explain why those emitted events don't appear in the runner's event log — bridge.lock.json seems to confirm a distinct process there. That would mean the "Goal set" message from initializeWorkerGoalSession happened after respawn but got logged elsewhere.

The likely root cause: the worker process was reaped while idle, and when the goal dispatcher hits a not-found agent, it just defers with "no_active_lease" and waits indefinitely for a worker to attach rather than triggering a respawn itself. Only the user's incoming prompt actually causes the respawn, so the goal only gets delivered as a side effect of that — meanwhile the UI status flipped to "pursuing" on retry even though the original dispatch was silently refused, which is misleading.

The fix should make the goal dispatcher resume/respawn the worker the same way a user message does when it discovers the lease is missing, then dispatch afterward. I want to trace how the direct message send path revives a stopped worker and check why the worker got reaped in the first place, possibly tied to a recent runner recovery change.

## Claude · 2026-09-26T20:47:13.217Z

The goal did reach the worker eventually, but only as a side effect of your message respawning it. Now finding how a user message revives a stopped worker, so a goal can use the same path.

```text
import { dbClient } from "@/server/db";
import { requireApiSession } from "@/server/auth/guards";
import { emitNamedEvent, type SurfacedErrorCode } from "@/server/events/named-events";
import { goalControl } from "@/server/runs/goal-control";
import { goalControlDispatchCoordinator, recoveryAction } from "@/server/runs/goal-control-dispatch";
import { goalOutboxDispatcher } from "@/server/runs/goal-outbox";
import { goalRateLimitManager } from "@/server/runs/goal-rate-limit";
import { redactGoalErrorMessage } from "@/server/runs/goal-errors";
import { attachGoalMutationToActiveWorker } from "@/server/runs/goal-worker-lease";
import {
  GOAL_ACTIONS,
  type GoalAction,
  type GoalMutationAction,
  type GoalMutationResult,
} from "@/shared/goal-plan";
import type { OmniHttpHandler } from "@/runtime/http/registry";

const MAX_GOAL_REQUEST_BYTES = 64 * 1024;
const MAX_IDENTIFIER_LENGTH = 128;

type MutationBody = {
  goalId: string;
  expectedRevision: number;
  operationId: string;
  objective?: string;
  action?: GoalAction;
};

function errorResponse(status: number, code: string, message: string, extra: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
  const goal = extra.goal;
  return Response.json({
    error: {
      code,
      message,
      ...(goal && typeof goal === "object" ? { details: { goal } } : {}),
    },
    ...extra,
  }, { status });
}

function nonEmptyIdentifier(value: unknown) {
  return typeof value === "string" && value.trim().length > 0 && value.length <= MAX_IDENTIFIER_LENGTH;
}

function parseMutationBody(value: unknown, method: string): MutationBody | null {
  if (!value || typeof value !== "object" || Array.isArray(value)) return null;
  const candidate = value as Record<string, unknown>;
  if (
    !nonEmptyIdentifier(candidate.goalId)
    || !nonEmptyIdentifier(candidate.operationId)
    || !Number.isSafeInteger(candidate.expectedRevision)
    || Number(candidate.expectedRevision) < 0
  ) {
    return null;
  }
  if (method === "PUT" && typeof candidate.objective !== "string") return null;
  if (method === "POST" && !GOAL_ACTIONS.includes(candidate.action as GoalAction)) return null;
  return {
    goalId: String(candidate.goalId).trim(),
    operationId: String(candidate.operationId).trim(),
    expectedRevision: Number(candidate.expectedRevision),
    ...(method === "PUT" ? { objective: candidate.objective as string } : {}),
    ...(method === "POST" ? { action: candidate.action as GoalAction } : {}),
  };
}

async function readJsonBody(request: Request) {
  const declaredLength = Number(request.headers.get("content-length") ?? 0);
  if (Number.isFinite(declaredLength) && declaredLength > MAX_GOAL_REQUEST_BYTES) {
    return { ok: false as const, response: errorResponse(413, "goal.payload_too_large", "The goal request is too large.") };
  }
  if (!request.headers.get("content-type")?.toLowerCase().includes("application/json")) {
    return { ok: false as const, response: errorResponse(415, "goal.payload.invalid", "The goal request must be JSON.") };
  }
  const text = await request.text();
  if (new TextEncoder().encode(text).byteLength > MAX_GOAL_REQUEST_BYTES) {
    return { ok: false as const, response: errorResponse(413, "goal.payload_too_large", "The goal request is too large.") };
  }
  try {
    return { ok: true as const, value: JSON.parse(text) as unknown };
  } catch {
    return { ok: false as const, response: errorResponse(400, "goal.payload.invalid", "The goal request is malformed.") };
  }
}

async function runExists(runId: string) {
  const result = await dbClient.execute({ sql: "SELECT id FROM runs WHERE id = ? LIMIT 1", args: [runId] });
  return Boolean(result.rows[0]);
}

function mutationFailureResponse(
  result: Extract<GoalMutationResult, { ok: false }>,
  details: { runId: string; goalId: string; operationId: string; action: GoalMutationAction },
) {
  const mapping: Record<typeof result.code, { status: number; publicCode: SurfacedErrorCode }> = {
    invalid_objective: { status: 400, publicCode: "goal.objective.invalid" },
    not_found: { status: 404, publicCode: "goal.persistence.failed" },
    revision_conflict: { status: 409, publicCode: "goal.revision_conflict" },
    operation_conflict: { status: 409, publicCode: "goal.revision_conflict" },
    invalid_transition: { status: 409, publicCode: "goal.transition.invalid" },
    stale_lease: { status: 409, publicCode: "goal.lease.stale" },
    unsupported_action: { status: 422, publicCode: "goal.action.unsupported" },
    provider_error: { status: 502, publicCode: "goal.acp.transport_failed" },
    persistence_error: { status: 500, publicCode: "goal.persistence.failed" },
  };
  const mapped = mapping[result.code];
  if (details.action === "set" || details.action === "edit") {
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "goal.set.refused",
      runId: details.runId,
      goalId: details.goalId,
      operationId: details.operationId,
      reason: result.code,
    });
  } else {
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "goal.action.refused",
      runId: details.runId,
      goalId: details.goalId,
      operationId: details.operationId,
      action: details.action,
      reason: result.code,
    });
  }
  emitNamedEvent({
    kind: "error.surfaced",
    code: mapped.publicCode,
    message: result.message,
    surface: "banner",
    runId: details.runId,
  });
  return Response.json({
    error: { code: mapped.publicCode, message: result.message, details: { goal: result.snapshot } },
    goal: result.snapshot,
  }, { status: mapped.status });
}

async function dispatchAndPublish(
  result: Extract<GoalMutationResult, { ok: true }>,
  action: GoalMutationAction,
  operationId: string,
) {
  const control = await goalControlDispatchCoordinator.dispatch(
    result.snapshot,
    result.replayed ? recoveryAction(result.snapshot) : action,
  );
  if (control.kind === "deferred") {
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "goal.reconciliation.refused",
      runId: control.snapshot.runId,
      goalId: control.snapshot.goalId,
      workerId: control.snapshot.workerId,
      reason: control.reason,
    });
  }
  if (control.kind !== "unsupported" && control.kind !== "superseded" && action !== "set" && action !== "edit") {
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "goal.action.completed",
        runId: control.snapshot.runId,
        goalId: control.snapshot.goalId,
        operationId,
        action,
        revision: control.snapshot.revision,
      });
  }
  return control;
}

export const handleGoalRequest: OmniHttpHandler = async (request, context) => {
  const runId = context.params?.id?.trim();
  if (!runId) return errorResponse(400, "goal.payload.invalid", "A run id is required.");

  const auth = await requireApiSession(request, {
    action: request.method === "GET" ? "Read goal" : "Change goal",
    source: "Goal",
    enforceSameOrigin: request.method !== "GET",
  });
  if (auth.response) return auth.response;

  let exists: boolean;
  try {
    exists = await runExists(runId);
  } catch (error) {
    const message = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "error.surfaced", code: "goal.persistence.failed", message, surface: "banner", runId });
    return errorResponse(500, "goal.persistence.failed", "The goal state could not be loaded.");
  }
  if (!exists) {
    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "error.surfaced", code: "goal.not_found", message: "The run does not exist.", surface: "banner", runId });
    return errorResponse(404, "goal.not_found", "The run does not exist.");
  }
  if (request.method === "GET") {
    return Response.json({ goal: await goalControl.getGoal(runId) });
  }
  if (request.method !== "PUT" && request.method !== "POST") {
    emitNamedEvent({ kind: "error.surfaced", code: "goal.payload.invalid", message: "The goal request method is not supported.", surface: "banner", runId });
    return errorResponse(405, "goal.method_not_allowed", "The goal request method is not supported.");
  }

  const parsedBody = await readJsonBody(request);
```

```text
import type { DbClient } from "@/server/db";
import { dbClient } from "@/server/db";
import { emitNamedEvent } from "@/server/events/named-events";
import { goalControl, type GoalControlService } from "@/server/runs/goal-control";
import type { GoalMutationResult } from "@/shared/goal-plan";

const ACTIVE_GOAL_WORKER_STATUSES = new Set([
  "starting",
  "working",
  "running",
  "idle",
  "stuck",
  "recovering",
]);

type GoalWorkerCandidate = {
  id: string;
  sessionId: string;
  status: string;
  workerNumber: number;
  createdAt: number;
};

function normalizeStatus(value: unknown) {
  return String(value ?? "").trim().toLowerCase().split(":")[0]?.trim() ?? "";
}

function rowToCandidate(row: Record<string, unknown>): GoalWorkerCandidate | null {
  const id = typeof row.id === "string" ? row.id.trim() : "";
  const sessionId = typeof row.bridge_session_id === "string" ? row.bridge_session_id.trim() : "";
  const status = normalizeStatus(row.status);
  if (!id || !sessionId || !ACTIVE_GOAL_WORKER_STATUSES.has(status)) return null;
  return {
    id,
    sessionId,
    status,
    workerNumber: Number.isFinite(Number(row.worker_number)) ? Number(row.worker_number) : 0,
    createdAt: Number.isFinite(Number(row.created_at)) ? Number(row.created_at) : 0,
  };
}

function compareCandidates(left: GoalWorkerCandidate, right: GoalWorkerCandidate) {
  return right.workerNumber - left.workerNumber
    || right.createdAt - left.createdAt
    || right.id.localeCompare(left.id);
}

export async function selectActiveGoalWorker(
  client: Pick<DbClient, "execute">,
  runId: string,
  currentWorkerId: string | null,
  currentSessionId: string | null,
) {
  const result = await client.execute({
    sql: `SELECT id, status, bridge_session_id, worker_number, created_at
          FROM workers
          WHERE run_id = ? AND bridge_session_id IS NOT NULL`,
    args: [runId],
  });
  const candidates = result.rows
    .map((row) => rowToCandidate(row as Record<string, unknown>))
    .filter((candidate): candidate is GoalWorkerCandidate => candidate !== null)
    .sort(compareCandidates);
  return candidates.find((candidate) => (
    candidate.id === currentWorkerId && candidate.sessionId === currentSessionId
  )) ?? candidates[0] ?? null;
}

  } = {},
): Promise<Extract<GoalMutationResult, { ok: true }>> {
  if (result.snapshot.status === "cleared") return result;
  const client = options.client ?? dbClient;
  const control = options.control ?? goalControl;
  const worker = await selectActiveGoalWorker(
    client,
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:47:29.412Z

I'm examining resumeMissingDirectWorker and askDirectWorkerWithResume, thinking through the fix where goal dispatch resumes the worker when getAgent returns not_found in direct-mode. But I realize resuming triggers the manager's session init, which calls initializeWorkerGoalSession and could itself re-dispatch /goal, creating a potential loop I need to guard against.

I'm worried about a possible deadlock: if goal dispatch and the session-init dispatch both go through the same per-run coordinator queue in the same process, the second dispatch could get stuck waiting behind the first while the first is still awaiting it. This depends on whether spawnAgent goes through the same in-process coordinator or is actually an HTTP call to a separate bridge process, so I need to check the bridge-client implementation.

```text
export async function resumeMissingDirectWorker(
  run: RunRecord,
  worker: WorkerRecord,
  expectedTurnGeneration?: number,
) {
  const workerPredicate = expectedTurnGeneration === undefined
    ? eq(workers.id, worker.id)
    : and(
      eq(workers.id, worker.id),
      eq(workers.turnGeneration, expectedTurnGeneration),
    );
  let sessionId = worker.bridgeSessionId?.trim();
  let sessionMode = worker.bridgeSessionMode?.trim();
  if (!sessionId) {
    const metadata = await readWorkerSessionMetadata(worker.runId, worker.id);
    if (metadata) {
      sessionId = metadata.sessionId;
      sessionMode = metadata.sessionMode ?? sessionMode;
      await db.update(workers).set({
        bridgeSessionId: metadata.sessionId,
        bridgeSessionMode: metadata.sessionMode ?? worker.bridgeSessionMode,
        updatedAt: new Date(),
      }).where(workerPredicate);
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "worker.session_metadata_repaired",
        runId: worker.runId,
        workerId: worker.id,
      });
    }
  }
  if (!sessionId) {
    const message = `Direct worker ${worker.id} is missing persisted ACP session metadata.`;
    emitNamedEvent({
      kind: "error.surfaced",
    }
  }
}

async function askDirectWorkerWithResume(
```

```text
export async function spawnAgent(params: {
  type: string;
  cwd: string;
  name: string;
  mode?: string;
  env?: Record<string, string>;
  credentialProfile?: string;
  accountId?: string | null;
  model?: string;
  effort?: string;
  skillRoots?: string[];
  mcpServers?: BridgeMcpServer[];
  resumeSessionId?: string;
  credentialSource?: ClaudeGatewayCredentialSource;
}) {
  const gateway = await prepareClaudeGatewayLaunch({ ...params, workerId: params.name });
  const normalizedModel = gateway?.rawModel ?? (params.model ? normalizeModelForWorkerType(params.type, params.model) : undefined);
  const { model: _model, ...restParams } = params;
  const normalizedParams = {
    ...restParams,
    ...(normalizedModel ? { model: normalizedModel } : {}),
    ...(gateway ? {
      accountId: null,
      credentialSource: gateway.credentialSource,
      env: { ...(params.env ?? {}), ...gateway.environment },
    } : {}),
  };

  return requestBridge<AgentRecord>(
    "/agents",
    {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify(normalizedParams),
    },
    "Spawn",
  );
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:47:58.904Z

Both runner and bridge share the same process, communicating over HTTP to the same port. If the goal dispatch path holds the per-run queue while awaiting a spawn that itself awaits an init dispatch queued behind it, that's a deadlock — so I'm tracing how GoalAcpDispatcher structures this sequencing.

Actually, on missing agent, the fix is to trigger resume outside the coordinator's queue rather than inside it — a fire-and-forget or deferred call after returning, so session init's initializeWorkerGoalSession can deliver the goal cleanly once the lease attaches. But I still need to figure out why the worker disappeared in the first place: the runner started well before the conversation existed, and the worker went idle sometime after — I should check whether the bridge reaps idle agents.

## Claude · 2026-09-26T20:47:58.912Z

Runner and bridge share one process, so a naive "resume inside the dispatch" would deadlock on the per-run queue. Checking why the idle worker disappeared, then I'll design the fix.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:48:30.950Z

This confirms it: idle agents get reaped after 30 minutes by design, and while the send-message path resumes them properly, the goal-setting path doesn't—meaning any goal set on a conversation idle longer than 30 minutes gets silently dropped until the next user message. Now I need to fix this in the goals route's dispatch logic, handling the case where control is deferred due to no active lease but a worker still exists in the database.

The fix should call a resume helper that loads the run and worker, verifies direct mode and non-cancelled state, then resumes the missing worker and reinitializes its goal session to attach a lease and dispatch. I'm weighing whether to await this in the HTTP handler or do it as fire-and-forget background work with error surfacing, since spawning Claude can take a few seconds and I want the response to stay fast—similar to how the send-message path already handles this. I also need to verify this resume path actually triggers the goal dispatch correctly, since earlier logs showed a respawn led to a goal.updated event appearing, suggesting the mechanism works when exercised this way.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:49:03.607Z

The goal turn is currently running, so the user's "Fix all of the bugs" prompt is probably queued behind it since the spawn call is blocked by the init dispatch—once the goal turn finishes, that message will get delivered as a follow-up prompt. I should double-check whether spawnAgent actually blocks on manager.spawn awaiting the worker goal session initialization before proceeding.

Yes, it does block: the coordinator dispatch runs through sendSlashCommand and streams over HTTP until the turn completes, so spawning is stuck for the entire goal turn duration. This creates a second bug where session-init goal dispatch blocks spawn, similar to the plan startup issue, though that's separate from what I'm tracking.

This complicates my fix for resume-on-goal too—if I call resumeMissingDirectWorker in the background, spawn still blocks until the goal turn finishes, and its DB writes (status, session ids) only land after that. Meanwhile the worker's DB status during this window is unclear, so the UI might not reflect "working" correctly unless I explicitly set that status myself. The cleaner fix is probably to prevent the goal dispatch during session init from blocking in the first place.

Let me scope this down: the reported bug just needs the minimal fix of reviving the worker when goal dispatch finds no live worker, the same way a user message does — since resume triggers session init which triggers goal dispatch automatically, that revival should suffice. I'll run the spawn in the background rather than blocking, and I need to check how worker status gets synced from the bridge's live agent state to make sure "working" status is set correctly during this revival path.

I consider an alternative: dispatching the goal as a "/goal ..." user message like the manual path, but that risks a double-dispatch since session init already runs the goal on spawn before the message send happens — so that approach would duplicate the goal. Background revival via the existing resume path stays the cleanest fix.

I'm now checking who actually flips the worker status to "working" during the goal turn — it's unclear whether the send-message path sets it before the resume call or whether sync.ts's live catalog polling maps runtime bridge-agent state onto the DB row. That would explain the discrepancy, but I still need to confirm whether spawn truly blocks on the goal turn by checking if initializeWorkerGoalSession is awaited.

Confirming that the spawn call is awaited in the HTTP handler, which means the whole PUT /goal request would hang for the entire turn duration — that seems like a real blocking issue worth flagging, so I want to trace the rest of the route handler to see how it's actually structured.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:49:15.369Z

I'm noting that holding the HTTP request open for the whole turn is a pre-existing design issue, not something to fix now, though it could leave the UI stuck showing a spinner during goal-setting—worth flagging but not changing scope. I'm moving on to implement the dispatchAndPublish logic in goals.ts, handling the deferred case appropriately.

Adding a background revival helper in a new goal-worker-revival module that checks the snapshot state, verifies the run and worker exist, skips supervised or cancelled runs, and resumes a missing direct worker if conditions are met. I still need to check whether new event kinds need to be added to the typed union in named-events.ts, and review the lifecycle-observability doc for concurrency requirements.

I'm also considering the concurrency case where two messages arrive at once and both trigger resumeMissingDirectWorker — spawnAgent already handles this via isConcurrentAgentStartError with a waitForConcurrentAgentStart fallback, so that should be fine. But I'm less sure about what worker status shows in the DB during revival, since the spawn call blocks until the goal turn completes, and the UI might not reflect a "thinking" state properly during that window. I want to check whether sync.ts updates worker status from runtime state to confirm this.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:49:35.062Z

I'm planning a new helper in goal-worker-revival.ts that emits named events for revival started, completed, and skipped states, plus failure handling that surfaces a reconciliation-failed error. I'm now checking how the revived session's goal actually gets delivered to the worker on initialization.

For paused goals with no worker, I'm concluding revival isn't needed since nothing has to be delivered — those cases just get skipped. I need to decide where exactly to trigger revival: within dispatchAndPublish when the control is deferred due to no active lease, but I'm wary that adding revival to the startup recovery path too could cause many workers to spawn at once, so that needs some restriction.

I'm limiting revival to only pending or pursuing goals, and reasoning that at startup most goals with prior control already have a non-null control_method, so the risk there is minimal but still keeping revival to the user-initiated route for safety. I also need to skip revival if the snapshot has no workerId, meaning there's no worker at all in the run.

Since resumeMissingDirectWorker blocks on the goal turn completing via a blocking dispatch, I have to run the revival in the background using a fire-and-forget call with its own try/catch that emits events on failure — bare silent catches are bugs. The revival will still enqueue onto the same per-run coordinator queue as the session init dispatch, which should be fine since it runs after the route's own call returns.

Now I want to check resumeMissingDirectWorker's exact signature — it likely takes a RunRecord and WorkerRecord from the db schema types — and confirm whether it throws or returns null on failure, so I'll look at the surrounding code around those line ranges.

```text
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { db } from "@/server/db";
import { plans, runGoalOperations, runGoalOutbox, runGoals, runs, workers } from "@/server/db/schema";
import { handleGoalRequest } from "@/runtime/http/routes/goals";
import { __resetNamedEventsForTests, getNamedEventsSince } from "@/server/events/named-events";

const runId = "goal-api-run";

  getAgent: vi.fn(),
  invokeAgentAcpMethod: vi.fn(),
  askAgent: vi.fn(),
}));

vi.mock("@/server/bridge-client", () => bridgeMocks);

function request(method: string, body?: unknown, id = runId) {
  return new Request(`http://localhost/api/runs/${id}/goal${method === "POST" ? "/actions" : ""}`, {
    method,
    headers: method === "GET" ? undefined : { "content-type": "application/json", origin: "http://localhost" },
    body: body === undefined ? undefined : JSON.stringify(body),
  });
}

async function call(method: string, body?: unknown, id = runId) {
  return handleGoalRequest(request(method, body, id), { surface: "test", params: { id } });
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/runs/goal-worker-revival.ts",
  "content": "import { eq } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { db } from \"@/server/db\";\nimport { runs, workers } from \"@/server/db/schema\";\nimport { emitNamedEvent } from \"@/server/events/named-events\";\nimport { redactGoalErrorMessage } from \"@/server/runs/goal-errors\";\nimport type { GoalSnapshot } from \"@/shared/goal-plan\";\n\ntype RunRecord = typeof runs.$inferSelect;\ntype WorkerRecord = typeof workers.$inferSelect;\n\ntype RevivalDependencies = {\n  loadRun(runId: string): Promise<RunRecord | undefined>;\n  loadWorker(workerId: string): Promise<WorkerRecord | undefined>;\n  resumeWorker(run: RunRecord, worker: WorkerRecord): Promise<unknown>;\n};\n\nconst defaultDependencies: RevivalDependencies = {\n  loadRun: (runId) => db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get(),\n  loadWorker: (workerId) => db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get(),\n  resumeWorker: async (run, worker) => {\n    const { resumeMissingDirectWorker } = await import(\"@/server/conversations/send-message\");\n    return resumeMissingDirectWorker(run, worker);\n  },\n};\n\nconst CANCELLED_STATUSES = new Set([\"cancelled\", \"canceled\"]);\n\nfunction normalizedStatus(value: string | null | undefined) {\n  return String(value ?? \"\").trim().toLowerCase().split(\":\")[0]?.trim() ?? \"\";\n}\n\nconst inFlight = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();\n\n/**\n * Bring back the leased worker of a goal the runtime could not deliver.\n *\n * The runtime reaps idle agents, so a goal set on a conversation that sat idle\n * found no live worker and was parked as `no_active_lease` until the user sent\n * another message. That message is what used to resume the worker, and the\n * session initialization that follows is what delivers the goal. Resuming here\n * gives the goal the same path without waiting for the user.\n *\n * Runs in the background: the resumed session delivers the goal while it\n * initializes and the runtime holds that spawn open for the goal's first\n * turn, so the caller must not await it.\n */\nexport function reviveGoalWorker(\n  snapshot: GoalSnapshot,\n  dependencies: RevivalDependencies = defaultDependencies,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const pending = inFlight.get(snapshot.runId);\n  if (pending) return pending;\n  const revival = revive(snapshot, dependencies).finally(() => {\n    inFlight.delete(snapshot.runId);\n  });\n  inFlight.set(snapshot.runId, revival);\n  return revival;\n}\n\nasync function revive(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, dependencies: RevivalDependencies) {\n  const skip = (reason: string) => {\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.worker_revival.skipped\",\n      runId: snapshot.runId,\n      goalId: snapshot.goalId,\n      workerId: snapshot.workerId,\n      reason,\n    });\n  };\n  if (snapshot.status !== \"pending\" && snapshot.status !== \"pursuing\") return skip(`goal_${snapshot.status}`);\n  if (!snapshot.workerId) return skip(\"no_leased_worker\");\n\n  try {\n    const [run, worker] = await Promise.all([\n      dependencies.loadRun(snapshot.runId),\n      dependencies.loadWorker(snapshot.workerId),\n    ]);\n    if (!run || !worker || worker.runId !== run.id) return skip(\"worker_absent\");\n    // Supervised runs own their workers' lifecycle; only a direct conversation\n    // resumes its worker on behalf of the user.\n    if (run.mode !== \"direct\" && run.mode !== \"commit\") return skip(\"not_direct_run\");\n    if (CANCELLED_STATUSES.has(normalizedStatus(run.status))) return skip(\"run_cancelled\");\n    if (CANCELLED_STATUSES.has(normalizedStatus(worker.status))) return skip(\"worker_cancelled\");\n\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.worker_revival.started\",\n      runId: snapshot.runId,\n      goalId: snapshot.goalId,\n      workerId: worker.id,\n    });\n    await dependencies.resumeWorker(run, worker);\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.worker_revival.completed\",\n      runId: snapshot.runId,\n      goalId: snapshot.goalId,\n      workerId: worker.id,\n    });\n  } catch (error) {\n    const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.reconciliation.failed\",\n      runId: snapshot.runId,\n      goalId: snapshot.goalId,\n      workerId: snapshot.workerId,\n      reason,\n    });\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"error.surfaced\",\n      code: \"goal.reconciliation.failed\",\n      message: reason,\n      surface: \"banner\",\n      runId: snapshot.runId,\n      workerId: snapshot.workerId,\n    });\n  }\n}\n"
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-26T20:51:31.509Z

Lint is clean. The runner uses tsx without a watch mode, so changes only take effect on restart, and since the user's session is active I shouldn't restart it myself — I'll just mention this. I haven't live-tested end-to-end, so I should flag that too, along with the secondary issue that retry incorrectly flips status to "pursuing" and emits action.completed even when nothing was actually delivered, which misleads the UI.

Since session-init dispatch uses a blocking askAgent, the spawn holds the whole goal turn open, meaning the user's "Fix all of the bugs" message is likely queued and won't be delivered until the goal turn ends — I can't fully confirm this from the worker stream, so I'll hedge it as "likely." I also want to briefly note the goal.payload_rejected invalid_status error from Claude's 20:43:54 update, and that startup recovery intentionally doesn't revive dead sessions. Since the user didn't ask me to commit, I won't — time to write up a concise final answer.

## Claude · 2026-09-26T20:51:36.321Z

A goal set on a conversation that has been idle for a while never reaches the worker. I've fixed that, but the fix isn't live until the runner restarts.

**What happened in 838cee98af1d**
- The worker finished its last turn at 19:02. The agent runtime shuts down agents that have been idle for 30 minutes, so by 20:43 no worker process was running. The database still listed the worker as `idle` with its saved session.
- At 20:43:22 you set the goal. The server linked it to that worker, tried to send it, found the worker gone and gave up with `no_active_lease`. It then waited for "the next worker to attach", and nothing ever starts a worker just for a goal.
- At 20:43:38 you pressed Retry and got the same refusal. Even so, the goal status changed to `pursuing` and a `goal.action.completed` event was recorded, so the UI looked as though it had worked.
- At 20:43:53 you sent "Fix all of the bugs" as a message. The message path does restart a missing worker, and when the restarted session started up it found the pending goal and sent `/goal fix all of the bugs`. That's where the "Goal set" line at 20:43:54 comes from.

**The fix**
- **New module** `src/server/runs/goal-worker-revival.ts`: when sending a goal fails with `no_active_lease` and the goal is linked to a worker, the goal route (`src/runtime/http/routes/goals.ts`) now restarts that worker in the background using `resumeMissingDirectWorker`. That's the same function a follow-up message uses. The restarted session then sends the goal the same way it did for you.
- **When it restarts a worker:** only for direct conversations, only for `pending` or `pursuing` goals, and never for cancelled runs or workers. If a goal and a message arrive together, they share one restart.
- **It can't run inside the send itself.** The runner and the agent runtime share a process, and while a worker starts up it sends the goal through the same per-conversation queue. Waiting for the restart inside that queue would deadlock, so the restart runs in the background after the send gives up.
- **Events:** it emits `goal.worker_revival.started`, `goal.worker_revival.completed` and `goal.worker_revival.skipped`. A failed restart raises a `goal.reconciliation.failed` banner.

**Tests:** there's a new unit test file, `tests/server/goal-worker-revival.test.ts`, and a new route test in `tests/runtime-api/goals.test.ts` that reproduces this session's failure. All five goal test files pass (39 tests), and lint shows no errors. I haven't tested it end to end against the live app, and I left the runner (pid 60276) alone because your session is still working. Nothing is committed.

**Related problems I found but didn't change:**
1. Retry reports success and sets `pursuing` even when nothing was sent. With the fix it will trigger a restart, but the status is still claimed before anything is delivered.
2. When a worker starts up and sends a goal, it waits for that whole goal turn to finish before reporting that it has started. Your "Fix all of the bugs" message is probably queued behind the goal turn and will arrive as a second prompt when that turn ends.
3. At 20:43:54 the server rejected a goal status update from the agent with `goal.payload_rejected` (`invalid_status`). The server doesn't accept one of the status values Claude sends.
4. Goals that were never delivered before a runner restart still aren't retried when the runner starts back up. I left that out on purpose, so a restart doesn't launch a batch of workers at once.

Want me to fix any of those?
